I know I haven’t felt right, but “sad or empty most of the time”… I guess I’d need to think about what you mean by that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. I feel bad a lot, but I’m not sure how to separate that from just feeling worn down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really. I used to paint, and I liked cooking big meals, and I’d run before jobs sometimes. I used to show up for theater rehearsals too. Now all of that just feels like chores from somebody else’s life. I don’t really care if I miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty?\n\nPatient: Yes. A lot. I keep thinking everything that went wrong is because of me—like my husband getting worse, the crew losing work, clients’ homes getting messed up. It feels like I ruin whatever I touch, and sometimes I think I deserve whatever happens to me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a year and two months now. It started a few months after my husband got hurt, and it just kind of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Like I said, I used to actually enjoy painting, cooking big meals, going for runs before work, even theater rehearsals. Now it all just feels like something I’m supposed to do, not something I want to do. Like it belongs to somebody else’s life, and I don’t really care if I miss any of it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I used to paint, and I liked cooking big meals, and I’d run before jobs sometimes. I used to show up for theater rehearsals too. Now all of that just feels like chores from somebody else’s life. I keep thinking everything that went wrong is because of me—like my husband getting worse, the crew losing work, clients’ homes getting messed up. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I keep having thoughts that it might be better if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes I feel like dying would be some kind of fair punishment for what I’ve done to my family and the people I work with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that. I know I haven’t felt right, but “sad or empty most of the time”… I guess I’d need to think about what you mean by that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. I feel bad a lot, but I’m not sure how to separate that from just feeling worn down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really. I used to paint, and I liked cooking big meals, and I’d run before jobs sometimes. I used to show up for theater rehearsals too. Now all of that just feels like chores from somebody else’s life. I don’t really care if I miss it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty?\n\nPatient: Yes. A lot. I keep thinking everything that went wrong is because of me—like my husband getting worse, the crew losing work, clients’ homes getting messed up. It feels like I ruin whatever I touch, and sometimes I think I deserve whatever happens to me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a year and two months now. It started a few months after my husband got hurt, and it just kind of stayed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Like I said, I used to actually enjoy painting, cooking big meals, going for runs before work, even theater rehearsals. Some weeks have been a little less crushing, I guess, but most of the time the guilt, the exhaustion, not caring about things, and those thoughts about death have still been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had more than one episode of feeling this way in your life?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not like this. My work pants have gotten loose.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two months where you didn't feel this way since it started?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had two months where it really lifted. It’s been going on about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband got injured. Some days are a little better than others, but it hasn’t gone away for that long.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where these feelings completely went away before returning?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It’s been there pretty much the whole time, for about a year and two months now, since a few months after my husband got hurt.
